California Masonic Memorial Temple – San Francisco

i

Larry L. Adamson

Grand Master
September 28, 2008 – October 4, 2009

ii

Larry L. Adamson

Grand Master – 2008-2009
A native Californian and fifth-generation Mason, Most Worshipful Larry
L. Adamson was raised a Master Mason on February 27, 1973, in Triangle Lodge
No. 548. He affiliated with his father’s Lodge, Palms-Adams No. 512, where he
served as its Master in 1989, and which later consolidated and became Sunset
Lodge No. 369 in Santa Monica. In 1998, he became a plural member of Old
West Lodge No. 813 in Newhall near his home.
Most Worshipful Adamson began his affiliation with our Craft when
he joined the Order of DeMolay in 1965. He is a Past Master Councilor, Past
League President, and holds the honors of Chevalier and the Legion of Honor.
Additionally, he served the order as a Chapter Advisor for five years and a League
Advisor for three years, is a Past Dean of the Southern California Preceptory,
and served for five years as the Associate Bethel Guardian of Bethel No. 249 in
Newhall, International Order of Jobs Daughters.
He has a distinguished career of service with our Grand Lodge, spanning
over twenty years. He has served as a Trustee of our Masonic Homes and was
the inaugural President of our Acacia Creek Adult Living Corporation. He
served Most Worshipful R. Stephen Doan as Grand Marshal in 1992, and Most
Worshipful David Decker as Grand Orator in 2000.
He was responsible for the restructuring of our leadership programs
during his tenure as Chairman of the Leadership Development Committee and
served as Chairman for our Sesquicentennial Year Committee and Chairman of
the Research Committee. He has served on many other committees and regularly
instructs at our Lodge Management Certification Workshops, Wardens Leadership
Retreats and Inspectors Seminars. He has also served on the California Masonic
Foundation, CMMT and Nob Hill Corporations.
Most Worshipful Adamson is a member of Eastern Star, the Scottish Rite,
York Rite and Shrine. In Scottish Rite, he was the Venerable Master of the Lodge
of Perfection in the Valley of Los Angeles, and in 2003 was coroneted a 33rd
Degree Inspector General Honorary. He is a member of all three bodies of the
York Rite and served as Grand Orator for the Grand Council of Cryptic Masons in
2004. He is a member of St. Gabriel Conclave of the Red Cross of Constantine,
the San Juan de Los Angeles Council, the Allied Masonic Degrees, the National
Sojourners and Heroes of 76. He is a member of Al Malaikah Shrine Temple
where he currently serves on the Los Angeles Shrine Hospital Board. In addition,
he is a Past President of the Southern California Past Masters’ Association and a
member of the Royal Order of Scotland.

iii

A graduate of California State University, Los Angeles, Larry served as
President of the Statewide Alumni Council which represents the 23 campuses
of the California State University System, and in 1996 was named as its Alumni
Volunteer of the Year. In December, 2004, he was appointed to the very prestigious
position of Trustee of the California State University System which is responsible
for education policy and governance over the system’s 480,000 students.
He was District Chairman for the Los Angeles Council of the Boy
Scouts of America, and President of the Los Angeles Morning Rotary Club. In
January of 2005, the Los Angeles County Board of Supervisors confirmed Most
Worshipful Adamson to serve as Commissioner of the Los Angeles Homeless
Services Commission, which he chaired in 2007 and 2008.
Most Worshipful Adamson is currently the President and CEO of the
Midnight Mission, the preeminent social service agency serving the poor and
homeless in Los Angeles. Before that, he completed a very successful 23-year
career with the Automobile Club of Southern California where he retired as Vice
President of Administration and Automotive Services.
Larry and his wife Lynn met in high school and live in Santa Clarita, a
community just north of Los Angeles. They have been married for 35 years and
have two married daughters, both of whom are elementary school teachers and are
majority members of Jobs Daughters Bethel No. 249, Newhall. They have two
grandsons and a granddaughter, and the Most Worshipful is honored to have two
sons-in-law that are members of Old West Lodge No. 813 as well.

Free and Accepted Masons of the State of California, do hereby certify that the
following pages contain a true and faithful transcript of the proceedings of that
body at its One Hundred Sixtieth Annual Communication, commencing at the
City of San Francisco, Friday the Second of October, A.L. 6009 and terminating
on Sunday the Fourth of October, A.L. 6009.

I have hereunto appended my official signature and have affixed the seal of the
M\W\ Grand Lodge of Free and Accepted Masons of the State of California,
at the office of the Grand Secretary, this First day of January, in the year of Light,
two thousand and ten.

Grand Secretary

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

3

One Hundred Sixtieth
Annual Communication
Friday Afternoon, October 2, 2009
The 160 Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of Free and
Accepted Masons of the State of California was called to order by Grand Master
Larry L. Adamson on Friday, October 2, 2009.
th

Worshipful Rosser L. “Doc” Adamson, Master of Siminoff Daylight
Lodge No. 850, raised the opening session of Grand Lodge.
Reception of the Past Grand Masters
The Grand Master welcomed the brethren and directed the Grand Marshal
to present the Past Grand Masters of the Grand Lodge of California at the Altar.
The Grand Marshal then introduced each Past Grand Master, following which
the Grand Master, accompanied by the Deputy Grand Master, personally greeted
each one. The Grand Master asked the brethren to join him in saluting our Past
Grand Masters by the battery of three times three. The Past Grand Masters were
then escorted to seats reserved for them in the auditorium. The Grand Master then
directed the Grand Secretary to send to each absent Past Grand Master the usual
letter of greeting from the Grand Lodge.
Roll Call
The Grand Secretary was asked to call the roll of Grand Lodge Officers,
Past Grand Masters, and Past Grand Officers, and to ascertain if a constitutional
number was present to convene the Grand Lodge of California. The following
were present as indicated by their response to the calling of the roll:

that therefore a quorum was present as required for the transaction of business.

Saturday Morning, October 3, 2009
The Grand Lodge convened at 8:30 a.m., and after prayer by the Grand
Chaplain, the Grand Master noted that Grand Lodge was raised by Worshipful
Rio L. Santonil, Master of Torrance University Lodge No. 394; assisted by
Brother Gabriel Guiterrez, a member of Montebello-Whittier Lodge No. 323.
Introduction of the District Inspectors
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Marshal to escort into the
auditorium the District Inspectors. The Grand Master thanked the Inspectors for
their continued service to the Fraternity as the representative of the Grand Master
and presented each of them with a small token of appreciation.
Grand Oration
The Grand Master then called upon the Grand Orator, Worshipful
Russell E. Charvonia, who presented the Grand Oration (Appendix H).
Legislation
Grand Master Adamson then reminded the members of the established
rules and relinquished his chair to the Deputy Grand Master before presenting his
Decisions and Recommendations.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

13

After delivering his Decisions and Recommendations, the Grand Master
resumed his station. He reminded the brethren of the established rules and
protocols and proceeded with the legislation starting with Unfinished Business.
See page 20 for results.
At 11:30 a.m., the Grand Master asked the brethren to pause in the
deliberations for the annual Memorial Service.
Memorial Service
The Grand Master called on the Very Reverend Timothy A. Wood,
Grand Chaplain, to present a service of remembrance for our departed brethren.
“As we are assembled today as the Fraternity of Freemasonry, bonded
as Brothers in the family of Fraternal friendship and brotherly love, we pause to
look back on the passing of many of our brothers who have been called by the
all-devouring Scythe of Time and who are now gathered together in the glorious
celestial lodge above where our fathers have gone before us.
In this Masonic year, 2,277 of our brethren laid down their trowels to
end their earthly toils for a more glorious purpose. We reflect on their individual
accomplishments and the many sacrifices that they made for their families, their
communities, and for their country – all for the greater good of Freemasonry
guided and enlightened by the Supreme Wisdom of their God.
Their individual contributions enriched our lives and helped illuminate
for us, the proper path that we must take for eternal salvation. By being true
adherents to their Masonic teachings, we know that they are resting peacefully
now.
No tear of sorrow needs to be shed in their passing but rather a thankful
blessing passed from one brother to another rejoicing for the time that we did have
together, the memories that we shared all the while awaiting that call - where all
our Past Fathers will be joined together in the divine presence of our Supreme
Architect.
Among our brothers who passed this year were two current District
Inspectors and we will remember them now.”
James Leo Adams
Worshipful Brother James Leo Adams, known as Jay by his friends and
family, was born on October 12, 1933 in New Mexico. He was initiated an Entered
Apprentice Mason in Hiram-Takoma Lodge No. 10 in the District of Columbia on
August 1, 1984, Passed on September 19, 1984, and Raised to the Sublime Degree
of Master Mason on November 13, 1984. He affiliated with Golden Gate Speranza

14

Proceedings of the

Lodge No. 30 in San Francisco on September 7, 1993, where he served as Master
for five terms. He received the Hiram Award from the lodge in 2003. He was
Inspector of the 163rd and later the 329th Masonic Districts from January, 2007 to
May 31, 2009. Brother Adams entered into rest on August 3, 2009.
Billie Noel McNeer
Worshipful Brother Billie Noel McNeer was born on October 17, 1935
in Arkansas. He was initiated an Entered Apprentice Mason in Tyrian Lodge
No. 439 in Manteca on September 21, 1971, Passed on November 11, 1971,
and Raised to the Sublime Degree of Master Mason on February 1, 1972. He
affiliated with Imperial Lodge No. 390 on December 13, 1982, where he also
served as Master for five terms. He received the Hiram Award from the lodge in
1997. He was Inspector of the 499th and later the 929th Masonic Districts from
August, 2005 until his passing on June 5, 2009.
“Soft and safe to thee my brothers be thy resting place. Bright and
glorious be thy rising from it. Fragrant be the acacia sprig that there shall flourish!
May the earliest buds of spring unfold their beauties o’er thy resting place, and
there may the sweetness of the summer’s last rose linger longest! Though the
winds of Autumn may destroy the loveliness of their existence, yet the destruction
is not final, and in the springtime, they shall surely bloom again. So, in the bright
morning of the resurrection, thy spirit shall spring into newness of life and expand
in immortal beauty, in realms beyond the skies. Until then, our dear brothers, until
then, farewell!”
Amen.

Most Worshipful R. Stephen Doan, Past Grand Master, delivered a
eulogy for the late Past Grand Master, Most Worshipful Roy J. Henville.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

15

Eulogy

Roy J. Henville
Roy Jackson Henville, our 119th Grand Master of Masons in California,
was born on August 11, 1913 in Rockville, a small town in Bates County, Missouri,
just east of Fort Scott, Kansas and south of Kansas City. He was the oldest child
of Frank Henville and the former Mindia Jackson, thus Roy’s middle name. Roy
was born only a few miles from where his father was born. Roy’s grandfather,
Sampson Henville, was born in London, England and emigrated with his family
to Canada when he was two. Roy’s grandfather came to Missouri in 1875 where
he married and became a farmer. Roy’s father, Frank Henville, was a fireman on
the Southern Pacific Railroad and brought his young family to Los Angeles before
World War One. Roy had one sibling, a sister, Maxine, born in 1923.
Roy started school in Los Angeles, but his family thereafter moved to
South Pasadena. He graduated from South Pasadena High School in 1932. In
high school, Roy competed in track and established a cross country record which
stood for over twenty years.
Every life has its pivotal events. An early one for Roy happened in the
fourth grade where he met Clara Elinor Phares. Elinor’s family lived less than a
half mile from Roy and his family. Although Elinor was a few months older than
Roy and graduated from South Pasadena High School a year before him, they were
sweethearts since elementary school. Roy and Elinor married on March 7, 1935,
celebrating their fiftieth wedding anniversary during Roy’s year as Grand Master.
In the 1930’s, Roy worked as a salesman delivering Borden dairy products. His
route included the Hollywood Hills in Los Angeles.
Roy’s and Elinor’s first child, Alexandra Lee Henville, was born on
January 14, 1938, two months premature. Those of you familiar with the Henville
family know that his daughter’s name is Sandy, not Alexandra. How Alexandra
became Sandy was another pivotal event for Roy and Elinor. This story and the fact
that Roy rarely mentioned it speak to his judgment and humility. When Alexandra
was ten months old, Elinor read of a nationwide search to find an infant with a big
smile to play Baby Sandy in the upcoming Universal Pictures musical comedy, East
Side of Heaven, starring Bing Crosby and Joan Blondell. Hundreds of infants had
been considered for the part but none yet selected. After a few days of discussion
between Roy and Elinor, Roy left Alexandra’s pictures with the butler for Charles
Previn, one of his Borden customers. Previn was then the musical director for
Universal Pictures. Previn liked the Henville baby pictures. Alexandra was called
in for a screening and won the part. Baby Sandy was a huge success. Universal
saw her as its answer to Shirley Temple, and a series of seven Baby Sandy movies
followed. Thus did Alexandra become Baby Sandy on screen and in real life. She
appeared in Life magazine and was the cover story in Look magazine’s August 14,
1939 issue. There were Baby Sandy dolls, mugs, diapers, clothing and books.

16

Proceedings of the

Despite this fame and resulting fortune, Roy and Elinor were not drawn
into the Hollywood lifestyle. Unique among the parents of the many then popular
child actors, the Henvilles lived largely as they did before Sandy’s screen debut.
On the set, it was just Sandy and Elinor with none of the glitz surrounding other
child actors. Between her salary for the Baby Sandy movies and the royalties
from her product endorsements, Roy’s and Elinor’s daughter was in the highest
income tax bracket in America, yet Roy still got up early every morning and
delivered the milk for Borden. Borden did soon discover that Baby Sandy’s father
had talent too and moved him to its public relations department. In his new job,
Roy screened Borden publicity movies at lodge meetings, churches and schools
and talked about the company’s products.
After son Gary was born in 1941 and Baby Sandy did her last movie in
1942, the family left Hollywood behind and moved to Sacramento where Roy
took a job in food sales and later food brokerage. It was in Sacramento that
another pivotal event in Roy’s life occurred. He joined Three Pillars Lodge No.
613 in Sacramento on May 24, 1944. Business next took him to Fresno where he
affiliated with Las Palmas Lodge No. 366 on June 21, 1945. The family moved
back to South Pasadena in time for Sandy to graduate from South Pasadena
High School in 1955. The family subsequently moved to San Marino, and Roy
affiliated with San Marino Lodge No. 685 on August 1, 1955. Son Gary joined
San Marino DeMolay, and Roy served on the chapter’s advisory board. Roy
also became an officer in the lodge and was its Master in 1964. In 1969, he was
appointed Inspector of the 532nd Masonic District.
Another pivotal event in Roy’s life occurred on Thursday, October 12,
1978. It was the closing day of the 129th Annual Communication of our Grand
Lodge. In those days, Grand Lodge elections were the last item of business before
lunch on Thursday. The incoming Grand Lodge Officers had lunch together and
then were installed into their respective offices that afternoon. Roy came to that
lunch a bit bewildered. That morning, the incoming Grand Master decided to
make a change in the Assistant Grand Lecturer for the then Division V and asked
Roy to take the job. With just a few hours notice, Roy was installed into that
office.
Roy quickly proved an able administrator in his new position and
demonstrated to a wider audience his wit, humility, judgment and dedication.
He was approached about being the recommendee for election as Junior Grand
Warden but declined because of his age. He was already sixty-five and thought
that too old to start a four year commitment to the progressive, elective line.
Over time, Roy relented and was installed as Junior Grand Warden in 1981, also
serving on the Homes Board for the next three years. He became Grand Master
on October 11, 1984.
Roy returned to the Homes Board in October of 1988, serving three
consecutive three year terms. Roy was President of the Board for eight of those

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

17

nine years, a period of great change at the Masonic Homes, all guided under Roy’s
capable leadership. After retiring from the Homes Board in 1997, Roy served
four years on the Grand Lodge Committee on Retirement Pension Plan. Roy
turned eighty-nine years of age during his last year on a Grand Lodge committee,
yet Roy at sixty-five (almost a quarter of a century earlier) thought that he was
then too old to be Grand Master!
In recognition of his years of service to Freemasonry, Roy was honored
by our Grand Lodge as its Mason of the Year in 1997.
Roy joined the Scottish Rite in Fresno in 1946 and affiliated with
the Valley of Pasadena in 1960, serving as Venerable Master in 1976. He was
decorated with the KCCH in 1975 and coroneted a Thirty-Third in 1981. After
his year as Grand Master, Roy served as the SGIG’s Personal Representative for
the Valley of Pasadena.
Roy was a member of the Board of Directors of the Midnight Mission in
Los Angeles for eighteen years, three of those years as its President.
The Henvilles were early members of San Marino Congregational
Church where Roy served as an usher, Chairman of the Board of Deacons and
on the Board of Trustees. Not surprisingly, Roy was a popular scripture reader at
church services, his marvelous voice communicating the Word of God with power
and inspiration.
In March of 2002, Roy and Elinor sold their home in San Marino and
moved to the Masonic Home at Covina. While a resident at the Home in Covina,
Roy served a term as the residents’ representative to the Homes Board, the first
time that a former President of the Homes returned to board meetings representing
the residents.
Elinor passed away on May 25, 2003. After her passing, Roy enjoyed
the companionship of Georgette Shrimp, a fellow resident of the Home. Roy
passed away on February 13, 2009, at the age of ninety-five years, six months and
two days. He was survived by daughter Sandy, son Gary and two grandchildren.
Funeral services were held on February 18th at Loraine Avenue Baptist Church in
Glendora.
It would be hard to find a Mason better liked by more people than Roy.
He greeted everyone with a smile and made each person he met feel important.
His good judgment, personal commitment, humility, thoughtfulness for others,
wit and constant search for consensus made him a natural leader and mentor. His
service to Grand Lodge spanned almost fifty years. He will be greatly missed,
and the best tribute we can offer him is our commitment to imitate his example.

August 11, 1913 – February 13, 2009

Proceedings of the
Presentation of the Membership Development Award

It is no secret that interest in Masonry is growing. The best-selling
novels help! National magazines and newspapers have reported on what seems
to be ever-increasing interest in our fraternity. Lodge and Grand Lodge Web
sites are reflecting this level of interest resulting in over 2,100 applications for
membership this year. Each of our lodges conferred more degrees on average this
year than any year since 1978!
In 2003, we introduced the “Pass It On” program full of new resources for
lodges and members to use in sharing Freemasonry with their family, friends, and
colleagues. As part of that program, a goal was set for each lodge to raise at least
five new Master Masons a year. This year, over 100 lodges have accomplished
that feat. Each of those lodges will receive special recognition from the Grand
Lodge for their great work.
The Grand Master made a special presentation to San Diego Lodge No.
35 who during the last 12 months rasied a total of 26 Master Masons.
Presentation of the Leadership Training Awards
The Lodge Management Certification Program consists of five courses
that are conducted simultaneously at a particular location or online. The
courses include: Hall Association Management, Lodge Finance, Membership
Development, Program Planning, and Lodge Administration. Each course is a full
day in length. At the end of each course, participants must pass an examination
before they are given a certification in that course.
The Grand Master presented plaques to the following brothers who have
completed all five courses of the Lodge Management Certification Program:

Francesca Long
Miss California Job’s Daughter
I would like to first start off with a quote that a very wise Mason once
told me, “What a great time it is to be a Mason in California!” Most Worshipful
Adamson, Grand Lodge Officers, members, families, and friends of the Masonic
Family, looking out at the people I see before me, I can fully agree with you Most
Worshipful. And because of you Masons today, I can say with full confidence
on behalf of my sisters throughout the state, what a great time it is to be a Job’s
Daughter in California!
First off I would like to thank the Grand Lodge of California, Free and
Accepted Masons, for granting me the privilege of speaking here today. It is such a
great joy to speak to you today, and to represent the Job’s Daughters of California.
As Miss California Job’s Daughter it is my privilege and duty to do two of my
very favorite things, to encourage and promote the interests and welfare of my
Order, as well as to be the Daughter emissary to the adult organizations of our
great Masonic Family. Thank you again Most Worshipful Adamson, this will truly
be a moment in my life that I will never forget.
“Applied Freemasonry-Living the Virtues Today, Building for a Better
Tomorrow” What does that mean to you? As the Grand Master’s theme for the
year, it has been a constant reminder to the lodges and Masons of California. So
is that simply a challenging statement that you have left in the back of your mind
and looking pretty as a pin on your lapel, or have you stepped up to the Grand
Master’s call to apply your virtues to your life today, in the hope of building
a better tomorrow. As a Job’s Daughter, I can say that this challenge has been
put into action. In all of my full seven years as being a member of the Masonic
Family, I have never seen such involvement in lodges throughout California. As
a Past Honored Queen of Bethel No. 249 Newhall, I have always felt the support
of our lodge, Old West Lodge No. 813. But this past year I have seen that same
support in all the lodges I have come across, and more importantly have heard
about it from my sisters first hand. I believe it was Most Worshipful Adamson

34

Proceedings of the

who said that forty of the fifty Associate Bethel Guardian positions that needed to
be filled have been, and that could not have happened had not you, the California
Masons, stepped up and started living the virtues of Freemasonry in your lives
today. I know that a simple gesture of support may not seem much to you, but
to us in the youth organizations, it truly makes a difference. You are taking steps
toward building a better tomorrow, because as you support Job’s Daughters,
Rainbow Girls, and DeMolay, you are supporting the future. Not only of your
adult Masonic organizations, but of our world as we know it.
My theme as I travel this year goes hand and hand with what you have
been striving after. It is: Renew Faith, Renew Hope, Renew Love. Go Green!
Green has always been a symbol of growth. We as Job’s Daughters and the
other youth organizations, and Masons and the other adult organizations, are
dependent on each other to grow. In order to be successful in this endeavor for
our organizations, we must renew the faith in our Orders and God, renew hope
for our futures, and renew the love we have for each other. Thank you once again
for this opportunity, and I am confident that under the leadership of Most Worship
Adamson, you have all taken a step towards living a life of Applied Freemasonry.
My prayer is that as we as Job’s Daughters strive to be the “fairest in the land,”
that you as Free and Accepted Masons will continue to “make good men better.”
Thank You.
Following her remarks the Grand Master invited Kelsee Richmond,

Kieth Seith
Master Councilor
Most Worshipful Grand Master, distinguished east, friends and brothers
at this time I would like to introduce the corps of officers who traveled with me this
evening; Miss Beth Burns Northern California DeMolay Association Sweetheart,
Miss Allison Foster Northern California DeMolay Association Princess, Brother
Nav Sherghill Northern California DeMolay Association Senior Councilor and a
member of Lodi Lodge No. 256 and Brother Nolen Brown Northern California
DeMolay Association Junior Councilor and a Fellowcraft Mason of California
Lodge No. 1 in San Francisco. I bring greetings on behalf of the DeMolays,
Sweethearts and Advisors of the Northern California DeMolay Association; I
come to you not only as a DeMolay but as a member of Yolo Lodge No. 195
in Davis. We would like to thank the Most Worshipful Grand Master and his
Grand Lodge Officers as well as the Masons of California as a whole for all of

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

35

the support you have shown towards our DeMolay chapters. There has been a
significant increase of chapter advisors, Masonic involvement at our events and
Masonic enthusiasm to help make sure our orders grow and thrive since the year
began. This, in turn, has lead to many DeMolays in Northern California becoming
Masons to help give back to you, who have helped us. The help and support you
have shown us has made great strides and it is important that it continues. We need
your help as Chapter Advisors, support at our local chapter breakfasts, fundraisers
and activities and our membership efforts. The effect your involvement has had
on the membership of our order is evident with the start of three possible new
chapters; Santa Clara being the first to initiate 7-10 new members on October 10th
at our Grand Masters Class. This is due to the amazing efforts of Dad Ken Nagel
who helped start the chapter in its growth. Santa Clara Chapter, equipped with
a dedicated advisory board, has already begun attending many area events, local
chapter meetings and has begun to hold its own activities and “club” meetings
before it has even officially started.
We are a family, a Masonic family. We need to support each other, help
each other and work together to make a difference. The help and unity of the
Masonic family reminds me of a story. There once lived a father who was very
sad about a particular habit of his eight sons, they perpetually quarreled among
themselves; even for the silliest causes. The father advised the children and asked
them to live and work in unity but the sons could not keep their promise for long
and they would end up in an argument the next moment. One day the father,
decided to teach the sons the lesson of unity, and gathered together eight sticks.
Then he called the eldest son and asked him take one stick from the bundle and
break it. He took a stick and could easily break it. Then the father asked him
to take two sticks and asked the son to break them together. He could do that
easily as well. And the next time he could break even three sticks together. Now
the father asked the son to take the entire bundle of sticks and break it holding
together. But this time it was a hard task and one by one all the eight sons tried
their hands; but no one could break it. The father asked the children; “Did you
understand the lesson behind this?” No explanation was needed for them as all of
them understood what their father wanted them to learn, that ‘unity is power.’ We
can translate this into our lives as a Masonic Family. Our respective orders are
each a stick. United we are unbreakable in our causes and endeavors but divided
we will break easily. Together we have the power to achieve any goal we set our
minds to. We simply have to continue to stand united and strong and help each
other. Thank you again for all your support. Most Worshipful Grand Master at
this time I would like to present to you a gift on behalf of Northern California
DeMolay as a thank you for all of the support you have given your DeMolay
brothers.

The Grand Master then called on Miss Samantha Forcum, Grand Worthy

Samantha Forcum
Grand Worthy Advisor
Most Worshipful Grand Master, Officers and Members of Grand Lodge,
distinguished and honored guests and friends of California Rainbow. Thank you
for having me here this morning, it is truly an honor to be here with you.
Have you ever heard the saying “it takes a village to raise a child?” Well,
I believe this to be true, and I can tell you from personal experience. It all started
18 years ago when I was just a year old. I was the fifth of six children in a
hardworking family. My Dad was working two jobs, while my Mother stayed
home with the kids. She came from a big family, and after being diagnosed with a
chronic illness at the age of 18, she knew that raising a big family was her calling
as well. Or so she thought. After a few years of being a fulltime mother, she grew
tired of the lifestyle and decided it was not what she wanted after all. Sadly, she
walked away from my family and never looked back, leaving my father a single
Dad of six children.
Two things could have happened next. My father could have lost hope,
felt sorry for himself, and given up on his children. OR being raised in a Masonic
family, he could use the values he learned in DeMolay to pick himself up, stand
tall, and carry on with his life – and that is exactly what he did. Luckily, we have a
supportive family that he could turn to, and lucky for us, he was enough of a man
to ask for help. My Father sent my older brother and me to live with his sister,
while he could get his life back in order.
At our new home, we quickly became part of the family. Our cousins
became siblings, and our Aunt and Uncle became second parents. There, my
Aunt used the values she learned as a former Job’s Daughter, and enrolled me in
school, introduced me to God, and brought me to Rainbow. It wasn’t long before
I was initiated, and my family expanded even more. When I joined the Masonic
family, I gained a thousand new parents, and thousands more siblings, not just in
Rainbow, but also in Job‘s Daughters and DeMolay.
The family of freemasonry embraced my entire family, as my older sister
became a Rainbow Past Grand Officer, my younger sister became a Past Worthy
Advisor, and my brother became a Past Master Councilor. Not to mention, my
Aunt became a member of Eastern Star, and my father, my hero, became one of
your fellow brothers, when he became a Mason.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

37

So, I tell you this story not because I am ashamed of my past, but because
I am proud of my future, and the dreams that the Masonic family has helped me
achieve. Like many parents, the Grand Lodge of California has helped me pay
for college, allotting a scholarship of ten thousand dollars a year for my studies at
UC Santa Barbara.
They say that seeing is believing, and now that I’ve seen it, I am a firm
believer that it does indeed take a village to raise a child. As for me, I am the
lucky one, because I had a whole state raising me! So today I would like to say
thank you to the California Masonic family. Not only did you raise my parents,
but together, you have raised me. And by teaching me the virtues to live by, I will
spend my whole life building for a better tomorrow.
The Grand Master thanked Samantha for her remarks and presented a
gift to her from himself and his wife, Lynn. The Grand Master also presented a
donation on behalf of the California Masonic Foundation to benefit the Rainbow
for Girls leadership training program.
The Grand Master then called on Bro. Maxwell Shoemaker, Master
Councilor, Southern California Jurisdiction, Order of DeMolay for his remarks.
Maxwell Shoemaker
Master Councilor
Most Worshipful Grand Master, Members of Grand Lodge, My Brothers
in DeMolay and Masonry, honored guests and friends, on behalf of Southern
California Jurisdiction, Order of DeMolay, I consider it a privilege to be given the
opportunity to address you at this Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of
California.
This past year, Most Worshipful Larry Adamson has been concentrating
on directing Grand Lodge’s support toward the youth orders. I have seen the
“Masons 4 Youth” website. It explains in great detail the different ways you can
get involved and support the Masonic Youth Orders. The website explains how,
and I quote, “The role of an advisor is fulfilling and rewarding – and one that is
vital today for the future of Masonry.” This is not a theory. I can tell you from
first-hand experience that supporting DeMolay directly influences the future of
Masonry. And the reverse is also true. A perfect example of Masonry’s influence
on DeMolay in the past is that our ritual was written by a Mason, and it is no
coincidence that most of our teachings and ceremonies reflect the basic tenets
of Freemasonry. As we look to the future, and with Masonry as our foundation,
DeMolay will continue to teach leadership and the importance and benefit of
taking responsibility, another hallmark of our Masonic heritage. As fraternal
organizations we clearly understand the value and the importance of Brotherhood.

38

Proceedings of the

It is in fact what gives us our strength as an organization. A humble reliance on
each other that benefits every member around the world. Aren’t these the qualities
we look for in our applicants for Lodge? As DeMolays reliability, humility,
responsibility, and brotherly love are all embedded in our Masonic DNA. From
our earliest days in our chapters, we are taught that these are the first stones laid in
the foundation of a good and valuable member of society. Among other virtues,
they are what true manhood is based upon. So with this as a backdrop, doesn’t it
make sense that DeMolays would be inclined to join the lodge that teaches them
how to properly use these working tools of manhood in everyday life?
Now there is no guarantee that a member of DeMolay will join the
Lodge, but I can tell you that DeMolay Advisors provide a profound influence for
the members, and becoming a mentor to these young men may surprise you to see
just how much they influence you in return. Many Advisors have told me that the
satisfaction they receive from assisting the youth comes from seeing these young
men grow into adults. When you take a young man under your wing, you are
participating in his transformation from a boy into a man. Believe it or not, as an
advisor you will be transformed as well. For when you take on the responsibility
of teaching young men how to be better men, it is necessary for you to become
a better man yourself. The advisors I spoke with described the experience as a
unique and rewarding one. By taking on the role of an advisor you are directly
and indirectly teaching them the purpose and benefits of Freemasonry. If you do
your job well, the rewards will be substantial. A young man will have learned
the values of Freemasonry. The community will gain a leader. And you will
gain personal satisfaction from having helped a fellow human being reach their
potential. When put into this perspective, I can guarantee DeMolays and their
friends into adulthood, will join your Lodge. I am a perfect example of that
principle. I myself am a 3rd generation Master Mason from the same Lodge as my
Father and Grandfather. I learned about the benefits and values of Freemasonry
from my father, as well as many other DeMolay Advisors. I was eager to join
the Lodge soon after I became of eligible age. But my interest in Masonry was
forged in my youth as a DeMolay and that love carries on into my adult years.
In fact, that love translated into action. A short time after I was raised, I was
honored to assist in raising my best friend and mentor from DeMolay. The honor
was even greater because he had done the same for me 6 years earlier when I was
initiated into my DeMolay Chapter. I believe that the same brotherhood that led
my friend to introduce me to the benefits of DeMolay, led me to return the favor
and introduce him to the even broader brotherhood of Freemasonry.
Since our inception, the interaction between DeMolays and Masonic
advisors has been mutually beneficial to both adult and youth. Every DeMolay
has the potential to go far, but in order for that to happen; our members need
positive role models who will support them and are willing to help them achieve
their goals.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

39

Being a DeMolay Advisor is a lot of work but it truly is rewarding. It
can be beneficial to our Chapters, your Lodges, and even enrich your own lives.
So I urge you to go out and help your local DeMolay Chapters by becoming a
DeMolay Advisor, because helping one of these fine young men reach the peak of
their potential, may be the light you have been seeking all along.

Terry Peters
Terry Peters got involved with Job’s Daughters in 1988 and
continued to support them for over 10 years. As a Senior
DeMolay, he decided to support DeMolay by joining the
Pleasanton Advisory Council in 1993. He immediately
jumped in to serve as Chapter Dad. Because of his mentoring,

bringing a struggling chapter to one of the strongest Chapters in

supports the 8 western states and is a voting member of the
DeMolay International Board of Directors.

Appendix A

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

49

Grand Master’s Message
Brethren of the Grand Lodge:
It is with the greatest of honor and pleasure that I welcome the delegates,
members and guests to this the 160th Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge
of California. My brothers, this is your Grand Lodge.
A little over a year ago, I knelt at this great altar and took the solemn
obligation of a Grand Master of California. Today, 370 days later, I stand before
you as enthusiastic and excited about our craft as I was then. As this is a great
time to be a Mason in California, it has been an even greater honor to have been
your Grand Master.
Section 404.130 (F) of the California Masonic Code requires the Grand
Master to present at each Annual Communication a written message setting forth
all his official acts during the year, the general condition of Masonry within this
jurisdiction and his decisions and recommendations of legislation that he deems
necessary or appropriate for the welfare of the craft. The detailed reporting of
my stewardship of these responsibilities and powers with which you invested me
will be found in the Proceedings of this Annual Communication. Later today
and tomorrow, I will separately present for your consideration the decisions and
recommendations which I have made or proposed. This afternoon, however, I
will report on the highlights from the detailed report, on the general condition
of our craft.
Let me begin my comments by mentioning something that I truly believe
is the reality today: “That this is a great time to be a Mason in California.”
My brothers, we have embarked upon an era of exciting progress and
unparalleled opportunity in California Freemasonry. The men knocking on the
doors of our lodges, and calling, and e-mailing and texting are a testimony to
this reality.
This year we saw record setting advancement in almost every
category worthy of measure. This year, we saw more men, nearly 2,200 apply
for admission to our lodges; the largest number of applicants in any year since
1987. Our constituent lodges, your lodges, had more degrees conferred per
lodge than in any year since 1978. Our lodges conferred more total degrees
during this period than in any time in the past twenty years. And, consider this
fact that in 1990 we had nearly 200 more lodges. Master Mason raisings, the
true barometer of the health of our Grand Lodge, increased to its highest level
since 1983.
Our craft is also getting younger. In 1999, just ten years ago, our
average age was 74 years. Today, ten years later, it is down to 64. This is

50

Proceedings of the

fueled by the fact that the average age of those new applicants is just 35 years.
The fastest growing segment of our membership is 18 to 27 years old, having
increased by over 300% in just the past three years.
The Great Architect of the Universe has also been kind to us during
this past year. The number of deaths reported is the lowest since 1941 and the
number of withdrawals, those who leave us voluntarily, is the lowest since 1905.
My brothers, this is a testimony to you and a new found sense of relevancy among
our brothers.
Our overall membership including Entered Apprentices, Fellowcrafts
and Master Masons stands at approximately 71,000. When I first was your
Junior Grand Warden Recommendee, the forecast was that when I took office
as Grand Master our Master Mason count would be under 50,000. Today, as
I close my term as Grand Master, our Master Mason count is at 57,500 – over
15% higher than predicted just five years ago. Yes, there is still a decline, but I
stand here today confident that before the end of this next decade, a future Grand
Master will stand here at this very podium and declare an end of decline and the
beginning of a new era of growth for California Freemasonry.
This year, through the hard work of two groups of brothers, I was
honored to issue two dispensations forming two new lodges in California. In
just a little while, you will have the opportunity to support the charter of one of
these lodges, Prometheus Lodge, under the number 851. I report this because it
is also part of the measure of the health of our jurisdiction and to inform you that
for the first time in five decades we will end this year with no net loss of lodges
in California.
With that said, however, I feel compelled to share with you the deep
concern I have about the health of a number of our lodges. As optimistic as I
am about our overall growth and direction, I am equally concerned about the
inequality of the prosperity that we are enjoying. While many of our lodges are
confronted with an explosion of men knocking at their doors, and are struggling
to keep up the pace necessary to move their candidates through the degrees, there
is a significant number of lodges, especially rural ones, that remain disengaged
and seemingly willing to accept as a fait accompli their surrender. My brothers,
that is a very unhealthy attitude and one that beckons our call. I strongly
recommend to our incoming Grand Master that much attention, support and
focused strategies be directed at this problem and as a jurisdiction, we together
look for solutions aimed at saving these lodges and preserving Freemasonry in
these communities.
This year we embarked on a very aggressive and highly-focused
strategic direction. I am pleased to report to you that we met and, in many
cases, exceeded our expected outcomes. Permit me to summarize some of our
accomplishments.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

51

First, the Rebuilding and Re-energizing of our Youth Orders. My
brothers, in our undertakings over these last nine months we have made incredible
strides – greater than I could ever imagine.
DeMolay, North and South, are the number one and two jurisdictions
in growth for all of DeMolay International with over 275 new initiates during
the year. Both have exceeded the goal set forth in my challenge to them at the
beginning of this year. In the South, three new chapters were formed and are
now fully-functioning. Two others have received their “letters of Temporary.”
Nine lodges have passed resolutions to sponsor a new chapter, with seven having
formed advisory boards and three others considering moving forward. The
percentage of Master Mason advisors has doubled this year, with almost one
hundred Master Masons joining the ranks as new advisors. This is the single
greatest increase in chapters and advisors since the 1970s.
In the North, it has been much the same. A new chapter was initiated in
Livermore and three new chapters are in the organizational stage. Additionally,
ten lodges have expressed interest in starting new chapters. Over 80 new Masonic
advisors have stepped up to add support to our Northern Jurisdiction.
In Rainbow, we have one new Assembly in formation and two more that
are in the inquiry stage. They have seen growth in Master Masons joining them
in an advisory role and visitation by lodges has been substantially increased.
Over 220 girls joined our assemblies in California during this past year – one of
the highest growth years in the decade.
In Job’s Daughters, I am saddened to report that we were not able to
make the strides that we have made with our other orders. Unfortunately, the
structure of their adult leadership seems to make progress difficult. With that
said however, I wish to thank a large number of you and your members for
answering my call to fill our role as advisors to our Bethels. As of today, less
than ten bethels remain without Associate Bethel Guardians and in each of these
cases a plan to accomplish this is moving forward.
I would like to take this opportunity to acknowledge the incredible
efforts of Most Worshipfuls Sorsabal and Decker for their leadership and “can
do” attitude in chairing our Youth Orders Initiative this year. Most Worshipful
Sirs, the craft is forever indebted to you both.
Progress on our other strategic initiatives has been outstanding. Through
the tireless efforts of three of our Assistant Grand Lecturers and two of our
Inspectors we completed the development and implementation of the Inspectors
Development Academy. Thirty one candidates completed this intense training
academy and now form the nucleus from which our future inspectoral leadership
will be selected. To Vern Dandridge, Jack Rose, Bud Ramsey, Randy Downey,
George Whitmore and the assistance of Brother James Banta from our Grand
Lodge staff, you have contributed immensely to the future success of our craft.

52

Proceedings of the

Our lost members initiative is progressing and continues to yield much
in return. Working through the immense backlog of 18,000 members without
current contact information, we have already located over 1,300 of the previously
unaccounted for. Efforts continue on the over 13,000 names remaining. Earlier
this year we began working directly with lodges to assist them in locating lost
members within their own databases. This new and powerful tool is proving
vital in supporting our strategic efforts and re-engaging those that we previously
had “let go.”
I am proud to report to you that this year we completed a major milestone
in our commitment to leadership development by completing all of the remaining
on-line management certification courses.
Strategic focus in strengthening management practices in our hall
associations was very evident this year. When we began the year, we had a large
number of hall associations that had been suspended by the Secretary of State,
the Franchise Tax Board or both. Through the efforts of our Masonic Property
Representative, a new Grand Lodge staff position, and the leadership of our hall
associations only nine such suspensions remain outstanding. In each case, the
necessary papers have been filed and all that remains is for the State to reinstate
them.
Last year, I told you that I was determined to use my position as Grand
Master to help chart a new course for the future. To “lift the veil of secrecy
from our venerable craft, allowing us to play a more active role in defining
Freemasonry to the world – rather than having the world ‘define us’ through
fiction and mystery.”
I wanted our Grand Lodge to take control over the way we as Masons
are “defined.” I wanted us to demonstrate important historical evidence that
our values, tenets and ideologies played a key role in developing important
institutions and constitutional democracies around the world.
Brethren, I stand before you today with an amazing story of success.
To date, we have raised more than $600,000 toward making this dream a reality.
We have seen an incredible outpouring of support for a partnership with UCLA
that we believe will transform how our craft is understood by the world. Look
for courses on Freemasonry and fraternal studies at UCLA later this year. When
you see or hear about them, stand proud in your knowledge that you had a hand
in their development. Stay tuned, my brethren, because this project has legs!
Thanks to all of you who have given your support to this initiative.
Member participation is also an indicator of our health and this year we
saw records set:
• Three hundred and fifty people attended our Masonic
Symposium at UCLA – three times more than any prior event.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

53

My deepest appreciation goes to Most Worshipful R. Stephen
Doan for his leadership and support in reaching this milestone.
• Three times more lodges competed in the Ritual Competition
than previous years.
• A doubling in those accessing our online training.
• The highest level ever in attendance at our Secretary’s Seminar.
• The highest level of philanthropic support through our Annual
Giving.
• This past November, over 350 Masons and their families
attended our first Veteran’s Day Observance here in this very
auditorium.
• Over 300 Masons and their families attended our Masons4Mitts
partnership presentation at AT&T Park in San Francisco with
the Community Foundation and Junior Giants Charities.
My year as Grand Master has been robust and active. I attended
over 300 events both in and out of our State. I had the honor of hosting the
Conference of Grand Masters this past February in Anaheim. I can with
prejudice say it was the best the Conference has ever seen. My gratitude goes
out to Most Worshipful David Doan and his planning committee and all of you
who volunteered your time to make it so very successful and who represented
California Freemasonry so admirably.
To all the lodges and our concordant organizations that welcomed
Lynn and me and my Grand Family you have provided us with a lifetime of very
wonderful memories and everlasting friendships.
The year however, was not without its challenges. When I took office
on September 28th of last year, I immediately continued the suspension of the
Charters of three lodges previously suspended by Most Worshipful Hopper:
Chula Vista No. 626, Torrance University No. 394 and Vacaville No. 134. I
am pleased to report to you that through the very focused and determined
efforts of the appointed Masters and officers and the dedication of the brethren,
I was pleased to return the charters of all three lodges during the year. My
sincere appreciation goes out to each of these lodges for their determination
and character.
Unfortunately, serious circumstances developed in two other lodges:
Lompoc-Santa Ynez No. 262 and Tujunga No. 592, which required me to
suspend their Charters on March 5 and April 6 respectively. Much hard work
awaits these two lodges before they can properly function independently. I
therefore recommend to our next Grand Master that these suspensions continue

54

Proceedings of the

and the eyes of the fraternity continue to watch and wise counsel continue to
guide them in their recovery.
Last year at our Annual Communication, you sent a clear mandate
regarding the Grand and Glorious Order of the Knights of the Creeping Serpents
that they could not operate in our jurisdiction as they had for the past several
years and that their failure to cooperate would no longer be tolerated.
After taking office, I took action to enforce your mandate. Some 411
suspected members of the Order were brought to my attention. All received an
official directive from me to either renounce their membership in the organization
and to agree to no further participation in the organization anywhere in the world
or to be charged and tried under the provisions of the California Masonic Code.
I am pleased to report to you that 387 have renounced and signed our
agreement. Five have been deferred from action as they are currently on active
military duty out of the country. One has already been tried and expelled and
the remaining I have referred to a lodge in this jurisdiction for filing of charges
of unmasonic conduct. That organization no longer exists in our jurisdiction.
However, in Southern California, a new leadership has come forward
and met with me and has developed a new set of Bylaws and a new structure
that prohibits degree conferring privileges. This newly structured entity is a
social club. This new social club is allowed under our Code and they understand
their need to focus on supporting our constitution and not circumventing it.
The leadership of this new social club has been entirely open and cooperative,
a refreshing change from the past. Those who have been a part of the old
organization in the North have not joined with those in the South and have not
indicated their desire to continue in any further activities.
In conjunction with this issue, after five years of impasse in attempting
to gain the cooperation of the Grand Lodge of the Philippines to halt the initiation
process of California Masons into the Snakes organization, I am pleased to report
that we now have a joint treaty from the Grand Lodge of the Philippines which
prohibits any member of their Grand Lodge from participating in the making of
a California Mason a member of the Grand and Glorious Order of the Knights of
the Creeping Serpents. Similar agreements are also now in place with the Grand
Lodges of Arizona and Baja, California.
Several months ago, it was brought to my attention that expelled Masons
from the Grand Lodge of Baja, California were active members of Al Bahr Shrine
and even held leadership roles in several Shrine Clubs under their authority. After
several attempts to gain their cooperation, I summoned the Imperial Recorder,
Potentate and Recorder of Al Bahr to meet with your Executive Committee and
account for their actions and inactions. As a result of that meeting, I am pleased
to announce that their newly found cooperative spirit has lead to the removal of
three members from membership in Al Bahr Shrine.

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

55

On a very positive note, I had the honor and privilege to award six 75
year Diamond Jubilee Awards to great Masons. A seventh was awarded on my
behalf by Most Worshipful Kirkpatrick and I thank you for your assistance. I
also had the privilege to present 50 year pins to two Past Grand Masters this year:
Most Worshipfuls Channon and Wordlow.
As you have probably surmised from my decisions and recommendation,
your Grand Master’s attention has been focused on our judicial system and its
application to circumstances that have come before me. Before you this afternoon
are several decisions that have guided me through the very complicated maze
of provisions governing this process. Over 30 pages, or just over 16% of the
entire content of the Masonic Code, deals with Masonic Trial procedure. It is
complicated and frankly unwieldy for me, so you can understand how challenging
it is for lodges who are only occasionally involved in it.
I have asked that our next Grand Master consider the appointment of
a special committee to review this portion of our Code and bring back to this
assembly a completely revised methodology and procedure for handling our
jurisprudence in California. My hope would be to see a system that places the
procedural burdens in the hands of those who are more expert in this field and
for a more expedited process that insures timeliness in dealing with situations. I
would hope for a fair but simplified approach. I trust you will look with favor on
this recommendation.
Permit me if you will to conclude my message today by acknowledging
those who have made my message today very exciting and rewarding for me.
First of all, to all of the lodge leaders, Inspectors and Grand Lodge Committee
members, your contributions have paved the way for much of the prosperity
that we are now enjoying. To this year’s Grand Lodge Family, I will be forever
indebted. Your unselfish dedication and commitment has fueled the engine that
has driven us so very far. You have been ever committed and loyal both to me
and to Lynn and have forged life-long friendships that will always be in our
hearts.
And a very special acknowledgement must go to Brian Bezner, the
Grand Marshal, and his wife, Stephanie, for their tremendous assistance all year
long.
To the Deputy Grand Master and Grand Wardens, your wise counsel
has been invaluable and your support of this year’s very aggressive agenda has
never wavered. My brothers, our future will be well served by these outstanding
Masons.
To the Grand Treasurer I must acknowledge your extraordinary
contributions this year. Your dedication to the financial health of the Grand
Lodge and all our entities, your dedication to the best and most efficient use of
our resources, your commitment to the financial health of our lodges and hall

56

Proceedings of the

associations and your wise counsel to our financial administration and to our
Investment Committee have insured that our Grand Lodge and entities remain
on very solid financial footing even through these challenging economic times.
I also want to express my deepest gratitude to our Grand Secretary and
the staff of the Grand Lodge. It has been my distinct honor and privilege to be
the first Grand Master to benefit from having his service for the entire span of my
year. His unparalleled management skills and his deep and abiding commitment
to our craft have made him an invaluable partner in accomplishing the strategic
goals of this past year. He has always been there to offer counsel and advice and
I am indebted to him for his unwavering support.
To brethren all, thank you for the trust and confidence you bestowed in
me by allowing me to be your Grand Master. Words cannot express the pride that
I have had every day representing California Freemasonry. You welcomed me,
accepted me, encouraged and supported me. What more could a man ask for?
Let me close by reminding each of us that our existence as Masons can
only be insured by our commitment to living our lives by the timeless virtues of
our ancient craft. Like many things, Freemasonry is never more than a generation
away from extinction.
Let us not be deterred from building our craft stronger in the future.
Remember, there are no constraints on a Mason’s mind, no walls around a
Mason’s spirit, and no barriers to our progress except those we ourselves erect.
you.

God bless our Ancient and Honorable Craft and each and every one of

Respectfully submitted,
Larry L. Adamson, Grand Master

Report of the Deputy Grand Master
2009/2010 Grand Lodge Budget
Kenneth G. Nagel, Deputy Grand Master

Each year your Deputy Grand Master is responsible for proposing a
budget adequate to fund the obligations of our Grand Lodge for the upcoming
fiscal year. Although this process has been refined over the last decade, it must
still begin a full four years before an actual budget is finalized and presented to the
Craft at our Annual Communication. The present system of budget projections
must include forecasted future inflationary factors, resources to support strategic
initiatives, and most importantly, the 2009 membership projections. In the past,
the financial staff has spent a substantial amount of time working with the
incoming Grand Officers and their expertise has been invaluable. This year was
no exception.
After completing many months of work on this process, it is my intent
to present to the Grand Lodge at our Annual Communication, a revenue and
expense budget of $2,563,307. The proposed budget, which is attached for your
review, has been thoroughly vetted by the Grand Lodge Finance Committee,
Grand Treasurer, and Grand Secretary. Each have met either individually or
collectively with me to review the proposed budget for 2009/2010 and all have
agreed with me that this is a fair representation of our financial needs to support
our future obligations. I have also had the opportunity to share this with the
Wardens and members who attended the Wardens Leadership Retreats over the
last two years. Their input is invaluable.
The revenue for the next fiscal year is estimated to be $2,563,307 of
which $1,815,205 is from per capita. The projected billable membership is 58,555,
which is less than anticipated at this time last year. The final audited membership
numbers will not be available for several weeks, but it appears the actual will be
close to the new projection. The distribution policy for withdrawals from our
investments was changed this year from 5% to 4.5%, so income from investment
withdrawals is down by approximately $26,000 from what was anticipated
when we presented the budget estimate last year. The dividend from the Nob
Hill Masonic Center will be $119,905 based on the profits of the corporation
for the fiscal year ending October 31, 2008. Other income includes revenue
from applications fees, a grant from the California Masonic Foundation, and
reimbursements from investments and Masonic Service Bureau expenses. These
revenues are explained further in the enclosed materials.
With lower revenues from per capita and investments, we have worked
to lower some of the expenses. Reductions were made in fraternal expenses,
appropriations, travel and wages. As a result the expenses for the upcoming fiscal
year are projected to be $2,510,493. After a minimal expense for depreciation,
I am proposing that we expense approximately $30,000 of the potential surplus

76

Proceedings of the

for the pension plan termination fund. A number of years ago, the Grand
Lodge changed the retirement program from a defined benefit plan to a defined
contribution plan and at some time in the future there will be an expense for
terminating the plan. The expenses are detailed on the attached budget and the
graphs that follow also illustrate expenses by category.
Your current Executive Committee has worked collaboratively on many
initiatives to ensure a seamless approach to our programs and adherence to
our Grand Lodge Strategic Plan as we transition from Grand Master to Grand
Master. My budget request includes your support of a number of these continuing
initiatives as well as three new strategic initiatives, which I believe are essential
to the immediate support of our lodges and the future well-being of our craft. We
are continuing our efforts to locate lost members and widows, provide support
to Hall Associations, publish six issues of the California Freemason magazine,
support our Youth Orders, and provide comprehensive leadership training.
We are seeing a true resurgence of interest in our Craft. Younger men
are knocking at our doors with a desire to join men of like-minded interests and
values to make a difference in their communities as well as their own lives. I
believe that our Craft is vital to our society, and California Freemasonry must be
ready to take on new challenges of the future. You and I as members of Grand
Lodge must remain focused if we are to meet those challenges that await us.
I think this budget request I am proposing will meet our needs. It is a “Grand
and Glorious day to be a Mason”, and your support will help to ensure that our
Masonic virtues remain alive and relevant for generations to come.

GRAND LODGE OF FREE AND ACCEPTED
MASONS OF CALIFORNIA
Fiscal Year 2009/2010
Kenneth Nagel, Deputy Grand Master
Revenue Analysis

The Grand Lodge has several sources of income. Historically, the primary revenue source has been per-capita and degree fees. The Grand Lodge
Endowment Fund was created in 1992 and generates investment income. To better manage those monies, a four and a half-percent spending policy
has been instituted, calculated on a rolling five-year valuation to ensure the preservation of the fund principle. The Grand Lodge may have other
income like interest on bank accounts and other financial instruments from time to time. In 2004, the California Masonic Foundation included in its
mission the funding of Masonic education and leadership training programs and now provides a grant for those purposes. In 2005, the delegates of
the 156th Annual Communication adopted a $30 application fee to be paid to Grand Lodge by all applicants for degrees and out-of-state affiliations.
The Grand Lodge manages different service bureaus throughout the state and receives reimbursement for those expenses. The Grand Lodge also
manages the investments of its related entities and receives reimbursement equal to the cost of those services. The Grand Lodge is the sole
shareholder of the Nob Hill Masonic Center Inc. and may receive dividends on the profits of the corporation.

Installation Address

Kenneth G. Nagel
Grand Master of Masons in California, 2009-2010
Good morning, and thank you for participating in this, our annual
celebration.
It is a grand and glorious day to be a Mason!
I expressed this sentiment two years ago to those attending our Junior
Wardens’ Retreat, who are now getting ready to take the mantels of their own
lodges. It was true then, and I believe it to be even more true today.
This weekend, we heard Most Worshipful Larry speak to the resurgence
of our craft. More men are seeking membership in our lodges than we have seen
in many years. Lodges are conferring degrees in record numbers, and the number
of those being raised to the Sublime Degree of Master Mason is unparalleled in
recent times.
This past year, through the leadership of Most Worshipful Larry, many
new initiatives commenced. I am committed to continuing the work that he began
this year, and will make sure that these programs continue to be a major part of our
strategic plan. Our hall associations are becoming stronger and more professional.
Through technology, the Internet, and new staff emphasis, many of our lost brethren
and widows have been found. And the leadership and management education of our
craft is second to none. The commencement of our new Inspector’s Development
Academy (the IDA) has already yielded great results. And Most Worshipful Larry’s
project of reconnecting with our university campuses, beginning with UCLA, is a
legacy that will continue for generations to come.
I’d like to draw your attention to one more initiative, which I pledge to
continue. The revitalization of our youth groups is in full swing. Many of our
brethren and our lodges have taken up the cause and are returning to a strong
relationship with our youth groups.
This cause is personally significant to me. Like the five grand masters
that preceded me, I, too, am proud to say that I am a Senior DeMolay. I married
my favorite Jobie, and earlier this year, I was proud to witness my granddaughter,
Kayla, initiated into the Rainbow Girls. And today is another special day. In about
eight hours, it will be exactly 40 years to the day – October 4, 1969 – that I was
installed Master Counselor of Santa Clara Chapter, Order of DeMolay. The gavel
that was used by Most Worshipful Sorsabal to install the corps of officers this
morning is the same gavel presented to me on that evening by my Mom and Dad. I
know they are looking down and are with me today.
I am proud to say that through the vision of my own master, Worshipful
Mike Hertzberg, a new DeMolay chapter is being formed and sponsored by my

86

Proceedings of the

lodge, Liberty No. 299. And next weekend, the first group of boys to make up that
chapter will be initiated at the Grand Master’s Class in Oakland. My brothers,
this new enthusiasm for our youth is spreading throughout our jurisdiction. Our
youth will be the beneficiaries, but so, too, will our lodges. I implore each of you
to become involved with our youth orders today. It will be an experience like
none other. Many have heard the adage, “We know not what the future holds,
but we know who holds our future.” Our youth groups matter! And I want each
of them to know that, like Most Worshipful Larry, this grand master will also
champion your cause.
My commitment to Masonry began with DeMolay, and it has grown
each day since. Looking ahead to this year as your grand master, I want to inspire
all of our members and lodges to feel the same way.
My theme for the coming year is, “Freemasonry, the Foundation of
our Country; the Blue Lodge, the Foundation of our Craft; Coming Home.”
Remember: Freemasonry shaped this country and the very system of democracy
that we enjoy today. And Freemasonry would not exist without our craft lodges.
The theme of Coming Home has two important meanings. First is the
physical act of Coming Home to our blue lodges. When I reflect on all I have
gotten out of my lodge and how grateful I am to have a community of men who
share the same values, I can’t help but think about my brothers who have lost
touch. In a typical lodge today, we are lucky if we see more than 15 percent of
our membership. My vision for this year is to bring the remaining 85 percent
back to our lodges.
Many have heard me say that “Masonry is a habit.” If you are in the
habit of attending lodge, then you are engaged, your lodge benefits from your
participation, and your own life is enriched. But, if you are out of the habit, then
it is easy to find a dozen excuses for not attending meetings or lodge functions.
Again, typically, 85 percent of our membership is not in the habit. This
year, we will work towards reversing that trend. We must find out what within
our lodge will prompt our members to become engaged.
We have many exceptional concordant and appendant organizations.
I am an active member in most. But I believe that in too many instances, we
have watered down our fraternity and stretched our membership too thin. Many
of those members attending other Masonic bodies have adopted an either/or
approach to membership, and they are not attending their blue lodges. But if our
craft lodges are not strong, our other organizations will flounder as well. I ask
each brother here today to examine your own habits. If you have not attended a
blue lodge meeting recently, and have not been active in your lodge, then it is
time to Come Home!
I envision a renewed enthusiasm and engagement in the work of our

Grand Lodge F. & A.M. of California

87

lodges and throughout our communities. We need to put forth whatever effort
it takes to reconnect our membership with our blue lodges. With the help of our
Membership Committee, I am working towards a membership call in February
2010. At February stated meetings throughout California, my goal is to achieve
the largest blue lodge gathering in decades.
In preparation for this special night, I am asking our lodge leadership
to take up the following challenge: See that your entire membership is contacted
by phone – and not just to leave a message, but to have a personal conversation
with each member. First, to ensure they are in good health. Second, to remind
them that they are valued members of your lodge and that they matter. Third, to
find out what would excite them about lodge again and how they can once again
become engaged with their brothers. And last, to convince them to come to a
special stated meeting during the same week as all other lodges in California.
On this same virtual night in February, we will start the process of member reengagement. This is much more than a one-time event: It will be the beginning
of our craft Coming Home.
It will be up to our lodge leadership to ensure that members find the
drive, the desire, and the motivation to continue Coming Home, today and in the
years ahead. You see, we don’t have a membership problem – we need only to
find a reason for our members to be engaged.
This brings me to the second connotation of my theme. I’ve talked about
members becoming engaged again. You see, I believe that “Coming Home”
means much more than just attending blue lodge. It is the inward meaning of
coming home to those ideals that we pledged at the altar during our initiation.
We need to Come Home to the fundamental values of Freemasonry, which help
us remain relevant and continue to thrive.
My brothers, I envision a time when all of our lodges take up this charge
– when every member is cared for, when all of our widows truly know we care,
when our community partnerships flourish under our support and involvement.
I feel passionately about this vision, and I ask you to join me in making it a
reality. Take it upon yourself to demand that your lodge applies the values of
Freemasonry each and every day.
I have a final vision to share for our year ahead. Spending 21 years in the
Navy, I have seen firsthand the efforts by our military men and women to keep us
safe and protect the freedoms we all enjoy today.
For my Grand Master’s Project, I will be asking all our members and
lodges to help me honor our veterans. There are two parts to this project. First,
there is a national Masonic Hospital Visitors Program, which we participate in
through the work of the Masonic Hospital Visitors Program Committee. In our
own jurisdiction, this committee has been co-chaired by Most Worshipful C. Ray
Whitaker in the North and Worshipful Bruce Ivie in the South, in concert with

88

Proceedings of the

our representative to that national committee, Most Worshipful Fred Sorsabal.
They, and all the brothers on this committee, do amazing things by visiting
our veterans at the 11 veteran’s hospitals and three veteran’s retirement homes
throughout California. I hope to raise funds for this committee to do even more
for our V.A. patients.
But I want to raise more than just money. I want to raise the awareness
of our membership. I want each one of us to become a committee of one, and
make an effort to visit our veterans. Let them know Masons care. Make their day
just a little brighter by reminding them that they have not been forgotten.
Sixteen million served in the United States armed forces during World
War II. As a way to say thank you to our greatest generation of veterans, my
Grand Master’s Project is also initiating a program called “Operation Greatest
Gift,” which is explained in this short video. Please watch.
I’m pleased to share that Brother Roy Kirkland and Brother Richard
Wing are here with us today. Brothers, I thank you for your contributions to this
project. Please stand so we can recognize you.
All veterans, please rise. Thank you for your service. It is greatly
appreciated. Now, will all the wives, mothers, fathers, sons, daughters, brothers,
sisters, or anyone that’s had a family member engaged in our armed forces please
rise. Thank you as well for your sacrifices. I know it was tough when your loved
one was not with you while serving their country.
This is an exciting time in our craft’s history. This year, we will honor
our veterans. We will support our youth. We will continue to thrive and advance
as a fraternity, and we will support and change the communities we live in. This
year, we will reach out to our brothers and reaffirm our fraternal ties. When we
Come Home in February – to the largest blue lodge gathering in decades – we
will prove our enduring commitment to each other and to our craft lodges. And
as we stand by our brothers and reflect on our fraternal vows, we will pledge
our continued commitment to Coming Home – every day, and every month, and
every year to come.
Yes, my brothers, it is a grand and glorious day to be a Mason. It is
time to Come Home – and, side by side with your brothers, enjoy all we have to
celebrate.
Thank you and God bless!
